# Operating constants

Every number mantle's production code runs with, and every number of the vendored crates on its
write path, has a row here: its kind, the value the code has, and what establishes it
(CLAUDE.md §4). The owner's rule, 2026-11-12: "We have a no ARBITRARY VALUES rule. Learned and
derived approaches or exceedingly well researched ONLY."
(docs/measurements/2026-11-12-arbitrary-values-audit.md is the audit this inventory starts from.)

A row's kind is one the rule accepts:

- **format**: a field width, tag, version, magic or length of an on-disk or wire encoding.
  Changing it changes the format.
- **external**: fixed by a specification or an interface (an RFC, the AWS S3 API reference, an
  operating system's interface, an algorithm's definition), or a stated property of a system
  mantle must meet (a stock client's part size or timeout).
- **derived**: computed by a stated model from measured inputs or structural facts, or a unit
  conversion. A value derived from a refused one is `inherited`, not derived.
- **cited**: prescribed by peer-reviewed work or a primary source, used as it prescribes.
- **learned**: measured by the running system; the row names what it measures.
- **counted**: bytes or items a structure allocates, counted by the counting allocator and
  pinned by a test: a property of the code, not of a machine.

or one it refuses, which makes the row an open item until the number is replaced:

- **picked**: set by hand, or a bound or a floor taken as the operating value.
- **knee**: read off one measured curve by a chosen criterion and frozen.
- **once**: measured once, on one machine.
- **copied**: another system's tuning.
- **guessed**: a guessed timeout, deadline or wait.
- **inherited**: derived from a refused input.
- **open**: nothing establishes it yet; the row says what would.

`scripts/check-contracts.py` reads every `const` and `static` holding a number, of any type, in
production code and in the vendored crates of its `VENDORED` list, and every literal parameter:
a number written as the whole value of a struct field, an assignment or a binding, a count or
duration argument (`with_capacity`, `sync_channel`, `take`, `Duration::from_*`, `min`, `max`
and the rest of its `CALLS`), a command-line default, or a size shift. It leaves out zero as a
parameter's value (the empty count, offset or sum), and one as `min`, `max` or `clamp`'s argument
or a `let` binding's (at least one; a count's first value). It fails when a number has no row,
when a row names no number, when a row's value is not the code's, and when a row of an accepted
kind states a basis the rule refuses (a knee, one machine's measurement, a picked value, another
system's tuning). `scripts/arbitrary-open.txt` lists the open items and only shrinks: CI runs the
gate with `--known scripts/arbitrary-open.txt`, which passes while the open rows are that list
and refuses a new one; a run without it fails on every open item.

A row names its number by file and key. A constant's key is its name. A literal parameter's is
`Type::function:slot`: the slot a field, a binding or a command-line flag (`--seconds`) by name,
a call with its argument (`take(4)`, `from_millis(500)`), a size shift in parentheses
(`(1 << 20)`), and `#2`, `#3` for the second and third of one key in one function. A value longer
than a hundred characters is stated as the SHA-256 of its tokens (`sha256:`).

Replace: derive from the volume's chunk count and the LSE count distribution at the durability target (Bairavasundaram et al., SIGMETRICS 2007) |

| `crates/chunk/src/writer.rs` `INCARNATION_RESERVE` | picked | `1 << 16` | 2^16, about 1,000 times the need at 11 segments a second by the same cost calculation (research 11 §11.3); audit §12.6 asks for the measured rate and an overhead goal. Refused (audit 2026-11-12): 2^16, '1,000 times the need' at an assumed 11 segments a second. Replace: as SEQUENCE_RESERVE, from the measured segment-open rate |
| `crates/chunk/src/writer.rs` `SEQUENCE_RESERVE` | picked | `1 << 24` | 2^24 keeps reservation cost under 10^-3 at the measured 4.7 ms flush (research 11 §11.3); audit §12.6 asks to parameterize it by the measured rate and fence cost. Refused (audit 2026-11-12): 2^24 from one measured 4.7 ms flush; audit §12.6 asks for the measured rate. Replace: reserve = measured sequence rate × the measured superblock flush time / the overhead goal, re-derived at start |
| `crates/client/src/lib.rs` `ATTEMPTS` | copied | `3` | Three attempts of a request each refused before a node took it: the AWS SDKs' standard retry mode's default `max_attempts` (docs/research/73). Refused (audit 2026-11-12): the AWS SDKs' default max_attempts copied: the bench counts a PUT failed after three refusals. Replace: retry while the retry quota (a learned token bucket) allows and the request's deadline holds; the server's SlowDown carries how long to wait (its measured drain time), as RFC 9110 Retry-After does |
| `crates/client/src/lib.rs` `PERSISTENT_CONGESTION_THRESHOLD` | external | `3` | `kPersistentCongestionThreshold`, three probe timeouts with no acknowledgement (RFC 9002 §7.6.1): the least an exchange is given once the client has measured its exchanges. |
| `crates/client/src/lib.rs` `PIECE` | derived | `hyper_transport::RECEIVE_CHUNK` | hyper-transport's `RECEIVE_CHUNK`: a put's source is read, and a reply's body taken, a receive chunk at a time. |
| `crates/client/src/lib.rs` `QUOTA` | copied | `500` | A client's retry quota, 500 units: botocore's standard retry mode's `INITIAL_CAPACITY` (`botocore/retries/quota.py`, read 2026-10-31), Brooker's local token bucket (research/77 [5], D4; docs/research/73). Refused (audit 2026-11-12): botocore's INITIAL_CAPACITY copied. Replace: size the bucket from the client's measured request rate and the server's measured refusal rate (Brooker's adaptive retry, the AWS SDK's adaptive mode, a CUBIC-paced rate learned from throttles) |
| `crates/client/src/lib.rs` `SLOWED_BASE` | copied | `Duration::from_secs(1)` | A slowed request's first backoff cap, 1 s: botocore's standard mode's `throttling_base_scale` (`ExponentialBackoff`, `botocore/retries/standard.py`, read 2026-10-31; docs/research/73). Refused (audit 2026-11-12): botocore's throttling base copied. Replace: back off from the server's stated drain (Retry-After from its measured queue) or from the measured probe timeout, doubling (RFC 6298 §5.5) with full jitter |
| `crates/client/src/lib.rs` `SLOWED_MOST` | copied | `Duration::from_secs(20)` | A slowed request's longest backoff cap, 20 s: botocore's standard mode's `_MAX_BACKOFF` (`botocore/retries/standard.py`, read 2026-10-31; docs/research/73). Refused (audit 2026-11-12): botocore's MAX_BACKOFF copied. Replace: cap at the request's own deadline, or the stock clients' patience it already carries |
| `crates/client/src/lib.rs` `SLOWED_RETRY` | copied | `5` | The quota a `SlowDown`'s retry takes, 5 units: botocore's standard mode's `_THROTTLING_RETRY_COST` (`botocore/retries/standard.py`, read 2026-10-31; docs/research/73). Refused (audit 2026-11-12): botocore's _THROTTLING_RETRY_COST copied. Replace: as QUOTA |
| `crates/client/src/lib.rs` `UNRETRIED_REFUND` | copied | `1` | The quota a request answered with no retry gives back, 1 unit: botocore's standard mode's `_NO_RETRY_INCREMENT` (`botocore/retries/standard.py`, read 2026-10-31; docs/research/73). Refused (audit 2026-11-12): botocore's _NO_RETRY_INCREMENT copied. Replace: as QUOTA |

| `crates/crc/src/lib.rs` `TOP` | derived | `1 <<($ bits - 1)` | `1 << (bits - 1)`: x^0 in the reflected register of the macro's width, as in zlib crc32.c `multmodp`/`x2nmodp`. |
| `crates/crc/src/lib.rs` `X2N` | format | `sha256:6261a91c5c4ac548` | x^(2^k) modulo the polynomial, for k in 0..bits. (a table of a format or algorithm (unlisted in constants.md: not a scalar)) |
| `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs` `P50_SAMPLES` | open | `16` | 16 from n ≥ 1.96²q(1−q)/δ² at δ = (1−q)/2 (research 11 §13.3); the 95% level and the δ rule are chosen, not cited. Refused (audit 2026-11-12): open in constants.md: the 95% level and δ = (1−q)/2 are chosen. Replace: take Le Boudec's order-statistic count for the quantile reported (Thm 2.1: 6 for a median at 95%), as measurement.md §5a does |
| `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs` `P99_SAMPLES` | open | `1522` | 1,522 from the same binomial normal approximation; the 95% confidence and δ = (1−q)/2 tolerance have no cited basis. Refused (audit 2026-11-12): open in constants.md (as P50_SAMPLES). Replace: Le Boudec's 368 for p99, 3,688 for p99.9 (measurement.md §5a) |
| `crates/disk/src/calibrate.rs` `STANDARD` | picked | `Self { min: 3, max: 6, precision: 0.05, }` | Three rounds are the fewest that estimate a spread; six are enough for their minimum and maximum to bracket the median 95% of the time (docs/research/11 §13.3). The precision, ±5%, is half the ±10% that comparisons between benchmark runs resolve (§16). Refused (audit 2026-11-12): min 3 (a spread needs 2), max 6 (min and max bracket the median 96.9% of the time: order statistics), precision ±5% as 'half the ±10% benchmarks resolve', a resolution asserted, not measured (measured A/A bands 13–953%); these rounds set the measured depth the issuer and reads run at. Replace: stop on the measured interval at the precision the consumer needs (the issuer's depth decision changes only when points differ by more than their intervals), with Georges et al.'s rule; take n from Le Boudec for the quantile used |

A filter whose key count is only bounded while its branch is built (a compaction's, duplicates merging) is made for the bound at the least count whose divisors each at most double the one before (OEIS A174973) and folded exactly, once the count is known, to the least divisor still giving its keys 10 bits: between 10 and 20 bits a key, where halving alone kept 40 at an eighth of the inputs (research/68 §1). Refused (audit 2026-11-12): RocksDB's default bits_per_key; the false-positive arithmetic is cited, the choice of 10 is RocksDB's tuning. | `crates/engine/src/trunk/mod.rs` `SHAPE_DEPTHS` | picked | `8` | 8, the depths `TrunkStats::shape` reports apart, each a fixed element, so taking the stats allocates nothing. Diagnostics only: no decision reads it. Refused (audit 2026-11-12): 8 depths reported, argued from fanout 8 (itself arbitrary). Replace: size from the trunk's own height bound at its fanout and leaf count |

| `crates/gateway/src/layout.rs` `CHUNK` | copied | `8 << 20` | Tectonic's "typically 8 MiB" chunk (docs/research/01 §1.14). docs/design/gateway.md says measured transfer sizing will replace it, and audit §12.6/§16.2 requires per-upload sizing. Replace: derive the chunk from the measured device and path: the size at which a chunk write reaches the device's sequential bandwidth (calibration) and a transfer's round trip is amortized (bandwidth-delay), within the memory each operation may hold; per upload size where the client states it (gateway.md) |

Numbers the benches' scripts and the measurement plan state, which the gate does not read.

| measurement.md §12 an A/A beside effects under about 5% | measured | An A/A of main's code, the layout alone moved by one function off every hot path, moved 100M phases 3-4%, one way in every round (research/61; research/70 [9]). |
| `scripts/doors-trials.sh` busy1x and busy2x, cpu1 and cpu2 (`busy_of`, `cpus_of`) | cited | Busy loops at one and two times the cores, 8 and 16 on CPUSET 8-15, and one and two CPUs, 8 and 8-9: one and two CPUs and a busy loop a core are CI's small hosts (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` `constrained`, `.github/workflows/bench-constrained.yml`); twice the cores is the plan's harder load (research/70 [21][23]). |
| `scripts/doors-trials.sh` memfloor's `MemoryMax` (`FLOOR`) | measured | 2,466,576,405 bytes, the cell's memory floor: three times the 822,192,135 bytes a node's `memory.floor.*` basis lines summed to as `mantle serve` at 4711a46a said them on the box, the floor it derives class by class (node.md §1.4; `crates/node/src/derive.rs` `floor`; research/70 [22]). |
| measurement.md §12 the relay's 500 ms one way | cited | The owner's condition for the native door (`vendor/hyper-quic/examples/geo_open_loop.rs`; research/07, `GEOGRAPHIC` 500 ± 100 ms; research/70 [19]). |
| `scripts/doors-trials.sh` `TAIL_ARGS` `--ops 3688` and `--bytes 512M` | derived | 3,688 operations a sized phase, the fewest that bound a p99.9 at 95% (measurement.md §5a), so a tail case reports a p99.9 at each of its sizes; 512 MiB a step, the least power of two above 3,688 objects of the largest tail size, 128 KiB (483,393,536 bytes), so the step's byte bound does not cut the operations. |
| `scripts/doors-trials.sh` `TAIL_ARGS` sizes 1 B, 64 KiB, 128 KiB | cited | The small sizes of step 1's list whose operations a busy box answers in under a second (the baseline, 2026-11-06): a byte, the layout's segment, S3's small-object line (measurement.md §12; research/70 [10]); the larger ones take 3,688 operations past a case's deadline. |

## What the open ones need

- The chunk writer's batch shape (`Limits`: 1,024 requests, 32 MiB, 4,096 fragments a chunk)
  and the index's 2^22 fragments: the derivations research 11 §5–§6 sets out, from a
  measured `T(bytes, records, depth)`, heap per entry, checkpoint bandwidth and a restart
  deadline. Calibration's `durable_large` copies the batch cap it should establish.
- Calibration's statistics (`P50_SAMPLES`, `P99_SAMPLES`, the rounds' counts and ±5%
  precision, the histogram's resolution and range, `commit::RATE_ONE`): a confidence level
  and tolerance from the service objective they serve, or from a cited source, and a round
  budget from the time a startup may take.
- A bucket policy's raw body limit (`policy::BODY_LIMIT`): a recording of how much white space
  S3 keeps in a policy it gives back as set.
- The benchmarks' ladders and step lengths: the regimes each exercises, in their
  measurement records, or step lengths from the samples their quantiles need
  (research 11 §16).
